<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove Bloch's conjecture for smooth connected projective complex surfaces with $`p_g=0`$: the Albanese homomorphism on integral degree-zero zero-cycles is an isomorphism.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-bloch)): Let $S$ be a smooth connected projective surface over $\mathbb C$ with $p_g(S)=q(S)=0$. Then $$\deg:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)\longrightarrow\mathbb Z$$ is an isomorphism, where $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)$ has integral coefficients.
  - Explanation: The integral degree map determines zero-cycle classes under vanishing geometric genus and irregularity.
- **C2** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-bloch-pg-zero)): Let $S$ be a smooth connected projective surface over $\mathbb C$ with $p_g(S)=0$. Then the Albanese homomorphism $$\operatorname{alb}_S:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)^0\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Alb}}(S)(\mathbb C)$$ is an isomorphism of abelian groups, where $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)$ has integral coefficients.
  - Explanation: The Albanese map determines integral degree-zero zero-cycle classes when the geometric genus vanishes.
- **C3** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-tate-motives)): Let $S$ be a smooth connected projective surface over $\mathbb C$ with $p_g(S)=0$. Then $h(S)_{\mathbb Q}$ is finite-dimensional in the sense of Kimura and $t_2(S)=0$.* *If, in addition, $q(S)=0$, then $$h(S)_{\mathbb Q}\simeq \mathbf 1\oplus\mathbb L^{\oplus b_2(S)}\oplus\mathbb L^2.$$ In this case, for every $m,n\ge0$, the rational Chow motives of the power $S^m$ and the Hilbert scheme $S^{[n]}$ of length-$n$ zero-dimensional subschemes are pure Tate, with $S^0=S^{[0]}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C$. For $Y=S^m$ or $Y=S^{[n]}$, the rational cycle-class maps satisfy $$\operatorname{cl}_Y^i:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^i(Y)_{\mathbb Q} \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}H^{2i}(Y,\mathbb Q), \qquad H^{2i+1}(Y,\mathbb Q)=0 \quad\text{for every }i\ge0.$$
  - Explanation: Vanishing geometric genus gives the stated rational motivic consequences; pure Tate conclusions additionally require vanishing irregularity.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-birational)): Let $\pi:\widetilde X\to X$ be the blowup of a smooth projective surface at a point. Then $p_g$ and $q$ are unchanged, and $$\pi_*:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(\widetilde X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(X)$$ is a degree-preserving isomorphism with integral coefficients. Consequently these assertions are invariant under birational maps between smooth projective surfaces.
  - Explanation: Point blowups preserve the surface invariants and integral zero-cycle group.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-reduction)): To prove Theorem <a href="#thm:bloch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bloch">1</a> with rational coefficients, it is enough to prove it for smooth minimal surfaces $X_0$ of general type with $p_g=q=0$. For such a surface, putting $k_0=K_{X_0}^2$, one has $$\label{eq:reduction-invariants} 1\le k_0\le9,\qquad \chi(\mathcal O_{X_0})=1,\qquad \int_{X_0}c_2(X_0)=12-k_0,\qquad b_2(X_0)=10-k_0.$$ It is also enough to prove the assertion on any chosen sequence of point blowups of each such $X_0$.
  - Explanation: The rational problem reduces to minimal general-type surfaces and can be studied on point blowups.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-der-arrow)): The fixed-target derived arrow stack has an open substack $\mathfrak U$ whose truncation $U$ is the ordinary Quot scheme with the chosen rank and Hilbert polynomial, before fixing determinant. On $U$, its tangent complex is naturally $$\label{der:unrestricted-tangent} \begin{aligned} T_{\mathfrak U}\|_U &\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Cone}}\bigl(R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S',S')\xrightarrow{i\circ-}R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S',V)\bigr)\\ &\simeq R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S',Q). \end{aligned}$$ It is perfect of Tor-amplitude $[0,1]$. These identifications are compatible with forgetting the source or target, with direct sums of arrows, and with base change. The same assertions hold for a fixed vector-bundle target of any rank and torsion quotients.
  - Explanation: The open derived arrow problem recovers the ordinary Quot scheme and its compatible tangent complex.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-der-truncation)): Let $\mathfrak Z$ be a locally geometric derived stack, locally of finite presentation, whose classical truncation $j:Z\to\mathfrak Z$ is a scheme. Suppose $E_Z=j^*L_{\mathfrak Z}$ is perfect of Tor-amplitude $[-1,0]$. The canonical map $$\phi_Z:E_Z\longrightarrow L_Z$$ has cone in $D^{\leq-2}(Z)$, and is a perfect obstruction theory. For a map of such enhancements, the induced cotangent map commutes with the corresponding maps $\phi_Z$.
  - Explanation: The truncation comparison supplies the specified obstruction morphism under its perfect-amplitude hypothesis.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-der-trace-surjectivity)): Let $0\to F\to V_0\to Q_0\to0$ be an exact sequence on $X$, where $V_0$ is a vector bundle of rank $r$ and $Q_0$ is torsion. The map $$\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^1(F,Q_0)\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Ext}}^2(F,F) \xrightarrow{\operatorname{tr}}H^2(\mathcal O_X)$$ is surjective if $p_g=0$. It is also surjective if $K$ is ample and $Kc_1(Q_0)>rK^2$.
  - Explanation: The determinant obstruction map is onto under either of the stated geometric hypotheses.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-quot-pot)): In either setting <span class="upright">(i)</span> or <span class="upright">(ii)</span>, the enhancement $\mathfrak B$ induces a perfect obstruction theory $E_B\to L_B$, with virtual tangent $$\label{eq:quot-tangent} E_B^\vee\simeq \mathop{\mathrm{fib}}\left\{ R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S',Q)\xrightarrow{d\det^o} R\Gamma(\mathcal I_o)[1]\otimes\mathcal O_B \right\}.$$ It is $G$-equivariant. Its virtual dimension is $$\label{eq:vdim} v=2n+(A-K)D.$$
  - Explanation: The based determinant fiber supplies an equivariant perfect obstruction theory and its virtual dimension.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-der-global-resolution)): The scheme $B$ has a $G$-equivariant closed embedding in a smooth projective scheme. Its obstruction theory has a global presentation by two $G$-equivariant vector bundles. The morphism to the truncated cotangent complex can be represented by a morphism of two-term complexes for this embedding.
  - Explanation: Global equivariant presentations permit the obstruction theory to be used in localization.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-fixed-pot)): The enhancement $\mathfrak W$ induces a perfect obstruction theory $E_W\to L_W$. For $k:W% % \mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=21AA}% \lhook\joinrel\rightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }% % B$, there is a natural isomorphism of obstruction theories $$\label{der:fixed-comparison} (Lk^*E_B)^{\mathrm{fix}}\xrightarrow{\sim}E_W,$$ compatible with their maps to $L_W$. Its virtual tangent is $$\label{der:fixed-tangent} E_W^\vee\simeq \mathop{\mathrm{fib}}\left\{ \bigoplus_{i=1}^2R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S'_i,Q_i) \longrightarrow R\Gamma(\mathcal I_o)[1]\otimes\mathcal O_W \right\}.$$ The moving virtual normal complex is $$\label{der:moving-normal} N_W^{\mathrm{vir}}=\bigoplus_{i\ne j}R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S'_i,Q_j),$$ where the $(i,j)$ summand has the difference between the characters on $L_j$ and $L_i$, or evaluated weight $w_j-w_i$.
  - Explanation: The fixed obstruction theory agrees with the fixed part of the ambient theory, including its obstruction morphism.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-rank-one-family)): Let $R$ be a noetherian parameter scheme and let $L_R\twoheadrightarrow Q$ be a flat family of rank-zero quotients of a line bundle on $X\times R$. Its kernel has a unique factorization $$S'=L_R(-\mathcal C)\mathcal I_Z,$$ where $\mathcal C$ is a relative effective Cartier divisor and $Z\subset X\times R$ is finite and flat over $R$. Both factors commute with arbitrary base change. Consequently, the rank-one Quot functor with specified divisor component and point length $r$ is represented by $$\label{fix:rank-one-product} U=\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}^P(X)\times X^{[r]}, \qquad S'=L(-\mathcal C)\mathcal I_Z.$$ The assertion includes nonreduced parameter schemes.
  - Explanation: Rank-one quotient families split uniquely into divisor and point data, including over nonreduced bases.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fix-trace)): On $X^{[r]}$, with universal ideal $\mathcal I$, there is a quasi-isomorphism $$\label{fix:hilbert-splitting} R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I) \simeq R\Gamma(\mathcal O_X)\oplus T_{X^{[r]}}[-1].$$ The two maps to the right-hand side are the trace and the inverse of the trace-free Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism. On $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}^P(X)\times X^{[r]}$, define $$\label{fix:point-bundle} F=\bigl(\pi_*\mathcal O_Z(K-\mathcal C)\bigr)^\vee.$$ It is a vector bundle of rank $r$, and relative Serre duality gives $$\label{fix:point-duality} R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal O_Z,\mathcal O(\mathcal C))=F[-2].$$
  - Explanation: Trace splitting separates the Hilbert tangent complex from the point obstruction bundle.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fix-smooth-euler)): Suppose $g:Y\to Z$ is smooth and carries a relative perfect obstruction theory $E_g\to L_{Y/Z}$ of amplitude $[-1,0]$. Then $\mathop{\mathrm{Ob}}_g=h^1(E_g^\vee)$ is a vector bundle, $$[\mathop{\mathrm{Ob}}_g]=[T_{Y/Z}]-[E_g^\vee]\quad\hbox{in }K^0(Y),$$ and its virtual pullback is $$\label{fix:smooth-operation} g^!\gamma=\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Ob}}_g)\cap g^*\gamma.$$ The formula holds over arbitrary base cycles. For $Z$ a point, it says $[Y]^\mathrm{vir}=\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Ob}}_Y)\cap[Y]$.
  - Explanation: For a smooth morphism the virtual pullback is ordinary pullback capped with the obstruction Euler class.
- **C15** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-fixed-pg0)): Assume $p_g=q=0$. A nonempty fixed part with lines $T,H$ and lengths satisfying <a href="#eq:length" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:length">[eq:length]</a> is the smooth product $$W=\|T\|\times\|H\|\times X^{[n_1]}\times X^{[n_2]}.$$ Let $x_i=c_1(\mathcal O_{\|D_i\|}(1))$, with $D_1=T,D_2=H$. The fixed virtual class is $$\label{fix:pg0-class} [W]^\mathrm{vir}= \prod_{i=1}^2\bigl(x_i^{h^1(D_i)}\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(F_i)\bigr)\cap[W], \qquad F_i=\bigl((K-D_i)^{[n_i]}\bigr)^\vee\otimes\mathcal O(1_i).$$ A part with either $m_T<0$ or $m_H<0$ contributes zero. For the other parts, after including $\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(F_1)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(F_2)$, pushforward over the linear systems is coefficient extraction at $[x_1^{m_T}x_2^{m_H}]$.
  - Explanation: The fixed virtual class reduces to Euler factors and coefficient extraction when geometric genus and irregularity vanish.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fix-relative-pot)): For the projection $f:W\to U_b$ to the unrestricted low rank-one Quot, there is a relative perfect obstruction theory whose virtual tangent is $$\label{fix:relative-tangent} T_f^\mathrm{vir}= \mathop{\mathrm{fib}}\!\left\{R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S'_a,Q_a) \longrightarrow R\Gamma(\mathcal I_o)[1]\right\}.$$ It is compatible with the absolute theories of $W$ and $U_b$, and $$\label{fix:relative-class} [W]^\mathrm{vir}=f^![U_b]^\mathrm{vir}.$$
  - Explanation: The high-factor determinant fiber gives a compatible relative obstruction theory over the low Quot factor.
- **C17** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fix-excess)): On the product $U_b=\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}^{D_b}(X)\times X^{[n_b]}$, the rank-one Quot virtual class is $$\label{fix:low-product} [U_b]^\mathrm{vir}= \mathop{\mathrm{e}}(F_b)\cap \bigl([\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}^{D_b}(X)]^\mathrm{vir}\times[X^{[n_b]}]\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: The point excess bundle multiplies the product divisor and Hilbert virtual class.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fix-cosection-support)): In the auxiliary setting a divisor component with $m_P<0$ has zero virtual class. If $m_P\ge0$, its virtual class is supported, by cosection localization, on the empty divisor or on the unique canonical divisor $z$. The empty-divisor class is the reduced point with coefficient one. Only the Picard component containing $K$ can give the canonical contribution.
  - Explanation: Cosection localization restricts the auxiliary divisor contribution to the empty and canonical divisors.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-fix-canonical-sign)): For the divisor component whose Picard component contains $K$, there is an integer $s>0$ such that $$\label{eq:canonical-cycle} [\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}^{K}(X)]^\mathrm{vir}=-s[z].$$ Here $\mathop{\mathrm{Div}}^{K}(X)$ allows variation of the line in its Picard component; $z$ is the unique effective canonical divisor.
  - Explanation: The canonical divisor contributes with a strictly negative virtual multiplicity.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-fixed-auxiliary)): Assume $p_g=q=1$, $K$ ample, and $D=3K$. Every fixed contribution vanishes except those obtained from a low divisor equal to the empty divisor or to the canonical divisor. In either surviving case the contribution is computed on the actual fiber $$\|D_a\|\times X^{[n_a]}\times X^{[n_b]},$$ with the factors $\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(F_a)\mathop{\mathrm{e}}(F_b)$. The low hyperplane is $x_b=0$ and $m_{D_b}=0$; pushforward over $\|D_a\|$ is extraction at $x_a^{m_{D_a}}$. The overall multiplier is $1$ for the empty divisor and $-s$, with the same $s>0$ as in <a href="#eq:canonical-cycle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:canonical-cycle">[eq:canonical-cycle]</a>, for the canonical divisor. The statement applies to operational insertions in universal perfect complexes, also after adjoining marked surface factors.
  - Explanation: Auxiliary fixed contributions are calculated after restriction to the actual empty or canonical divisor fibers.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rel-hilbert-recursion)): Let $r>0$, and let $N_r=X^{[r-1,r]}$ be the scheme of nested subschemes $Z'\subset Z$ of lengths $r-1,r$. Write $$\psi:N_r\longrightarrow X^{[r]},\quad \phi:N_r\longrightarrow X^{[r-1]},\quad \rho:N_r\longrightarrow X$$ for the two forgetful maps and the residual-point map. Then $N_r$ is smooth, projective, irreducible of dimension $2r$; $\psi_*[N_r]=r[X^{[r]}]$; and, with quotient-line convention, $$\sigma=(\phi,\rho):N_r =\mathbb P(\mathcal I_{r-1}) \longrightarrow X^{[r-1]}\times X.$$ Put $\mathcal L=\mathcal O_{N_r}(1)$ and $\zeta=c_1(\mathcal L)$. If $j:N_r% % \mathrel{% \BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=21AA}% \lhook\joinrel\rightarrow% \EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}% }% % N_r\times X$ is the graph of $\rho$, then $$0\longrightarrow(\psi\times\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\mathcal I_r \longrightarrow(\phi\times\mathop{\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\mathcal I_{r-1} \longrightarrow j_*\mathcal L\longrightarrow0. \label{rel:universal-ideal-sequence}$$ For a two-term vector bundle resolution $0\to U_1\to U_0\to\mathcal I_{r-1}\to0$ on $X^{[r-1]}\times X$, one has, for every $k\geq0$, $$\sigma_*(\zeta^k\cap[N_r]) =\left[\frac{c(U_1^\vee)}{c(U_0^\vee)}\right]_k \cap[X^{[r-1]}\times X]. \label{rel:projective-pushdown}$$ All these identities remain valid after adjoining other Hilbert or surface factors.
  - Explanation: Consecutive nested Hilbert schemes provide the universal ideal sequence and projective pushdown recursion.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-rel-marked-reduction)): Fix a finite list of line bundles on $X$. Consider a finite polynomial of Chern characters of universal point ideals and their derived duals, together with Chern classes of these lines and of $T_X$ pulled from the surface slots and equality diagonals between those slots, on $$X^{[n_1]}\times X^{[n_2]}\times X^\ell, \qquad \ell\geq2.$$ Keep two specified surface factors as outputs and push forward over the Hilbert factors and all other surface factors. The resulting Chow class is a finite rational linear combination of pushforwards of products of equality diagonals and line and tangent classes on powers of $X$. The coefficients and the list of such products can be chosen by one recursion independent of $X$ and of the chosen line bundles.
  - Explanation: The marked recursion eliminates Hilbert factors while retaining both surface outputs.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-universal)): The class in <a href="#rel:fiber-contribution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rel:fiber-contribution">[rel:fiber-contribution]</a> admits a decomposition $$C_*(T,H;n_1,n_2)=c_*[\Delta_X]+\Gamma_*, \qquad \Gamma_*\in\sum_{a+b=2}R_X^a\boxtimes R_X^b. \label{rel:universal-decomposition}$$ One can choose $c_*$ by a fixed universal rule. Besides the integer parameters and the chosen weights, this rule depends only on $$\int_X c_2(T_X),\qquad \int_X UV\quad (U,V\in\{K,L_1,L_2,T,H\}). \label{rel:numerical-data}$$ It gives $c_*=0$ when $n_1=n_2=0$. The same rule applies on every smooth projective surface with the same data and $\chi(\mathcal O_X)=1$, whether or not the lines $T,H$ are effective; effectivity is needed only for realizing the formula as a fixed contribution.
  - Explanation: A fixed formal rule separates a fiber contribution into a diagonal term and external products using numerical intersection data.
- **C24** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-chow-relation)): For either surface setting, and any $p\geq0$ with $v>p+2$, sum the classes <a href="#rel:fiber-contribution" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rel:fiber-contribution">[rel:fiber-contribution]</a> with the following multiplicities:* 1. *If $p_g=q=0$, sum over the effective ordered line pairs $T+H=D$ and nonnegative point lengths satisfying <a href="#eq:length" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:length">[eq:length]</a>, with $m_T,m_H\geq0$. Each fixed part has multiplier $1$.* 2. *In the auxiliary setting, $D=3K$. Sum over the ordered pairs $(3K,0),(0,3K)$ with multiplier $1$, and $(2K,K),(K,2K)$ with multiplier $-s$, with the admissible point lengths. Here $s>0$ is the canonical-divisor coefficient of <a href="#eq:canonical-cycle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:canonical-cycle">[eq:canonical-cycle]</a>.* *All sums are finite. Writing $c$ for the same weighted sum of the coefficients chosen in Proposition <a href="#prop:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:universal">23</a>, one obtains $$0=c[\Delta_X]+\Gamma \qquad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(X\times X), \label{rel:chow-identity}$$ where $\Gamma$ is a sum of external products of total codimension two.
  - Explanation: The weighted finite sum of fixed contributions yields the Chow relation.
- **C25** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rel-correspondence)): If $p_g=q=0$ and the coefficient $c$ in <a href="#rel:chow-identity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="rel:chow-identity">[rel:chow-identity]</a> is nonzero, then $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(X)^0\otimes\mathbb Q=0$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero diagonal coefficient forces rational degree-zero zero-cycles to vanish.
- **C26** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-nonrank)): Suppose $1\leq k_0\leq8$. Blow up $$e=\max\{k_0-5,0\}$$ distinct points of $X_0$, and call the resulting surface $X$. There are choices of $L_1,L_2,D,n,p$ on $X$ for which Theorem <a href="#thm:chow-relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:chow-relation">24</a> gives a relation with $c>0$. Consequently the degree map on $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(X_0)_{\mathbb Q}$ is an isomorphism.
  - Explanation: The lattice branch obtains a positive diagonal coefficient after the specified point blowups.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-nr-lattice-class)): If $\Lambda$ is odd, there is a line bundle $h_0$ on $X_0$ with $$h_0^2=-1,\qquad K_0h_0=1.$$ If $\Lambda$ is even, then $k_0=8$ and there is a line bundle $h_0$ with $$h_0^2=0,\qquad K_0h_0=2.$$
  - Explanation: The intersection lattice supplies a line bundle with the stated canonical pairing and self-intersection.
- **C28** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-nr-positive-pairing)): Let $P$ be an effective line bundle on $X$ such that $m_P\geq0$ and $\alpha P\ne0$. Then $KP>k$.
  - Explanation: An effective divisor detected by the test class has canonical intersection exceeding the canonical square.
- **C29** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-nr-surviving-numerics)): Every such fixed contribution with $\alpha H\ne0$ satisfies $$\label{nr:survivors} KH=H^2=k+1,\qquad n_1=n_2=0,\qquad m_H=0,\qquad m_T=p.$$
  - Explanation: The detected contributions have the stated intersections and empty point subschemes.
- **C30** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-nr-effective-pair)): The actual line bundles $$H=K+h',\qquad T=(d-1)K-h'$$ are effective and satisfy <a href="#nr:survivors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="nr:survivors">[nr:survivors]</a>, with $\alpha H\ne0$.
  - Explanation: The exhibited line bundles are effective, ensuring that a detected fixed pair occurs.
- **C31** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-rankone)): Let $X$ be a smooth minimal projective complex surface of general type with $p_g(X)=q(X)=0$ and $K_X^2=9$. Take $$\label{r1:data} L_1=L_2=0,\qquad D=3K_X,\qquad n=20,\qquad p=7.$$ The coefficient $c$ in the Chow relation of Theorem <a href="#thm:chow-relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:chow-relation">24</a> is strictly positive.
  - Explanation: The rank-one branch uses the specified Quot data to obtain a positive diagonal coefficient.
- **C32** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-r1-auxiliary-comparison)): For the common universal coefficients defined above, one has $$\label{r1:comparison} c_{\mathrm{out}}-s c_{\mathrm{mid}}=0, \qquad s>0,$$ where $-s$ is the canonical-divisor coefficient in Equation <a href="#eq:canonical-cycle" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:canonical-cycle">[eq:canonical-cycle]</a> for $Y$.
  - Explanation: The auxiliary surface compares the common outer and middle universal coefficients.
- **C33** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-middle-coefficient)): For the data <a href="#r1:data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="r1:data">[r1:data]</a>, with numerical inputs $K^2=9$ and $\int c_2=3$, the two-order middle coefficient is $$c_{\mathrm{mid}}=12.$$
  - Explanation: The length-two calculation gives the middle coefficient with both orders included.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-der-arrow",
        "exact_quote": "The fixed-target derived arrow stack has an open substack $\\mathfrak U$ whose truncation $U$ is the ordinary Quot scheme with the chosen rank and Hilbert polynomial, before fixing determinant. On $U$, its tangent complex is naturally $$\\label{der:unrestricted-tangent}\n\\begin{aligned}\nT_{\\mathfrak U}|_U\n&\\simeq\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cone}}\\bigl(R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(S',S')\\xrightarrow{i\\circ-}R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(S',V)\\bigr)\\\\\n&\\simeq R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(S',Q).\n\\end{aligned}$$ It is perfect of Tor-amplitude $[0,1]$. These identifications are compatible with forgetting the source or target, with direct sums of arrows, and with base change. The same assertions hold for a fixed vector-bundle target of any rank and torsion quotients.",
        "source_id": "PM-040.1"
      },

These identifications are compatible with forgetting the source or target, with direct sums of arrows, and with base change. The same assertions hold for a fixed vector-bundle target of any rank and torsion quotients.",

        "anchor": "stmt-der-truncation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathfrak Z$ be a locally geometric derived stack, locally of finite presentation, whose classical truncation $j:Z\\to\\mathfrak Z$ is a scheme. For a map of such enhancements, the induced cotangent map commutes with the corresponding maps $\\phi_Z$.",
        "source_id": "PM-040.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank-one-family",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a noetherian parameter scheme and let $L_R\\twoheadrightarrow Q$ be a flat family of rank-zero quotients of a line bundle on $X\\times R$. Its kernel has a unique factorization $$S'=L_R(-\\mathcal C)\\mathcal I_Z,$$ where $\\mathcal C$ is a relative effective Cartier divisor and $Z\\subset X\\times R$ is finite and flat over $R$. Both factors commute with arbitrary base change. Consequently, the rank-one Quot functor with specified divisor component and point length $r$ is represented by $$\\label{fix:rank-one-product}\n U=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}^P(X)\\times X^{[r]},\n \\qquad S'=L(-\\mathcal C)\\mathcal I_Z.$$ The assertion includes nonreduced parameter schemes.",
        "source_id": "PM-040.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rank-one-family",
          "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a noetherian parameter scheme and let $L_R\\twoheadrightarrow Q$ be a flat family of rank-zero quotients of a line bundle on $X\\times R$. Both factors commute with arbitrary base change. Consequently, the rank-one Quot functor with specified divisor component and point length $r$ is represented by $$\\label{fix:rank-one-product}\n U=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}^P(X)\\times X^{[r]},\n \\qquad S'=L(-\\mathcal C)\\mathcal I_Z.$$ The assertion includes nonreduced parameter schemes.",
          "source_id": "PM-040.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $R$ be a noetherian parameter scheme and let $L_R\\twoheadrightarrow Q$ be a flat family of rank-zero quotients of a line bundle on $X\\times R$. Both factors commute with arbitrary base change.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fix-trace",
        "exact_quote": "On $X^{[r]}$, with universal ideal $\\mathcal I$, there is a quasi-isomorphism $$\\label{fix:hilbert-splitting}\n R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(\\mathcal I,\\mathcal I)\n \\simeq R\\Gamma(\\mathcal O_X)\\oplus T_{X^{[r]}}[-1].$$ The two maps to the right-hand side are the trace and the inverse of the trace-free Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism. On $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}^P(X)\\times X^{[r]}$, define $$\\label{fix:point-bundle}\n F=\\bigl(\\pi_*\\mathcal O_Z(K-\\mathcal C)\\bigr)^\\vee.$$ It is a vector bundle of rank $r$, and relative Serre duality gives $$\\label{fix:point-duality}\n R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(\\mathcal O_Z,\\mathcal O(\\mathcal C))=F[-2].$$",
        "source_id": "PM-040.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-fix-trace",
          "exact_quote": "On $X^{[r]}$, with universal ideal $\\mathcal I$, there is a quasi-isomorphism $$\\label{fix:hilbert-splitting}\n R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(\\mathcal I,\\mathcal I)\n \\simeq R\\Gamma(\\mathcal O_X)\\oplus T_{X^{[r]}}[-1].$$ The two maps to the right-hand side are the trace and the inverse of the trace-free Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism. On $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}^P(X)\\times X^{[r]}$, define $$\\label{fix:point-bundle}\n F=\\bigl(\\pi_*\\mathcal O_Z(K-\\mathcal C)\\bigr)^\\vee.$$ It is a vector bundle of rank $r$, and relative Serre duality gives $$\\label{fix:point-duality}\n R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(\\mathcal O_Z,\\mathcal O(\\mathcal C))=F[-2].$$",
          "source_id": "PM-040.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On $X^{[r]}$, with universal ideal $\\mathcal I$, there is a quasi-isomorphism $$\\label{fix:hilbert-splitting}\n R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(\\mathcal I,\\mathcal I)\n \\simeq R\\Gamma(\\mathcal O_X)\\oplus T_{X^{[r]}}[-1].$$ The two maps to the right-hand side are the trace and the inverse of the trace-free Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism. On $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Div}}^P(X)\\times X^{[r]}$, define $$\\label{fix:point-bundle}\n F=\\bigl(\\pi_*\\mathcal O_Z(K-\\mathcal C)\\bigr)^\\vee.$$ It is a vector bundle of rank $r$, and relative Serre duality gives $$\\label{fix:point-duality}\n R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(\\mathcal O_Z,\\mathcal O(\\mathcal C))=F[-2].$$",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose $g:Y\\to Z$ is smooth and carries a relative perfect obstruction theory $E_g\\to L_{Y/Z}$ of amplitude $[-1,0]$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fix-relative-pot",
        "exact_quote": "For the projection $f:W\\to U_b$ to the unrestricted low rank-one Quot, there is a relative perfect obstruction theory whose virtual tangent is $$\\label{fix:relative-tangent}\n T_f^\\mathrm{vir}=\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{fib}}\\!\\left\\{R\\!\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}(S'_a,Q_a)\n       \\longrightarrow R\\Gamma(\\mathcal I_o)[1]\\right\\}.$$ It is compatible with the absolute theories of $W$ and $U_b$, and $$\\label{fix:relative-class}\n [W]^\\mathrm{vir}=f^![U_b]^\\mathrm{vir}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-040.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-fix-cosection-support",
        "exact_quote": "In the auxiliary setting a divisor component with $m_P<0$ has zero virtual class. If $m_P\\ge0$, its virtual class is supported, by cosection localization, on the empty divisor or on the unique canonical divisor $z$.         "source_id": "PM-040.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-fixed-auxiliary",
        "exact_quote": "Assume $p_g=q=1$, $K$ ample, and $D=3K$. Every fixed contribution vanishes except those obtained from a low divisor equal to the empty divisor or to the canonical divisor. In either surviving case the contribution is computed on the actual fiber $$|D_a|\\times X^{[n_a]}\\times X^{[n_b]},$$ with the factors $\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(F_a)\\mathop{\\mathrm{e}}(F_b)$. The low hyperplane is $x_b=0$ and $m_{D_b}=0$; pushforward over $|D_a|$ is extraction at $x_a^{m_{D_a}}$. The overall multiplier is $1$ for the empty divisor and $-s$, with the same $s>0$ as in <a href=\"#eq:canonical-cycle\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:canonical-cycle\">[eq:canonical-cycle]</a>, for the canonical divisor.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rel-hilbert-recursion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r>0$, and let $N_r=X^{[r-1,r]}$ be the scheme of nested subschemes $Z'\\subset Z$ of lengths $r-1,r$. Write $$\\psi:N_r\\longrightarrow X^{[r]},\\quad\n \\phi:N_r\\longrightarrow X^{[r-1]},\\quad\n \\rho:N_r\\longrightarrow X$$ for the two forgetful maps and the residual-point map. Then $N_r$ is smooth, projective, irreducible of dimension $2r$; $\\psi_*[N_r]=r[X^{[r]}]$; and, with quotient-line convention, $$\\sigma=(\\phi,\\rho):N_r\n =\\mathbb P(\\mathcal I_{r-1})\n \\longrightarrow X^{[r-1]}\\times X.$$ Put $\\mathcal L=\\mathcal O_{N_r}(1)$ and $\\zeta=c_1(\\mathcal L)$. If $j:N_r%\n  %\n  \\mathrel{%\n    \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=21AA}%\n    \\lhook\\joinrel\\rightarrow%\n    \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n  }%\n%\nN_r\\times X$ is the graph of $\\rho$, then $$0\\longrightarrow(\\psi\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\\mathcal I_r\n \\longrightarrow(\\phi\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\\mathcal I_{r-1}\n \\longrightarrow j_*\\mathcal L\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{rel:universal-ideal-sequence}$$ For a two-term vector bundle resolution $0\\to U_1\\to U_0\\to\\mathcal I_{r-1}\\to0$ on $X^{[r-1]}\\times X$, one has, for every $k\\geq0$, $$\\sigma_*(\\zeta^k\\cap[N_r])\n =\\left[\\frac{c(U_1^\\vee)}{c(U_0^\\vee)}\\right]_k\n \\cap[X^{[r-1]}\\times X].\n \\label{rel:projective-pushdown}$$ All these identities remain valid after adjoining other Hilbert or surface factors.",
        "source_id": "PM-040.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rel-hilbert-recursion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r>0$, and let $N_r=X^{[r-1,r]}$ be the scheme of nested subschemes $Z'\\subset Z$ of lengths $r-1,r$. Write $$\\psi:N_r\\longrightarrow X^{[r]},\\quad\n \\phi:N_r\\longrightarrow X^{[r-1]},\\quad\n \\rho:N_r\\longrightarrow X$$ for the two forgetful maps and the residual-point map. Then $N_r$ is smooth, projective, irreducible of dimension $2r$; $\\psi_*[N_r]=r[X^{[r]}]$; and, with quotient-line convention, $$\\sigma=(\\phi,\\rho):N_r\n =\\mathbb P(\\mathcal I_{r-1})\n \\longrightarrow X^{[r-1]}\\times X.$$ Put $\\mathcal L=\\mathcal O_{N_r}(1)$ and $\\zeta=c_1(\\mathcal L)$. If $j:N_r%\n  %\n  \\mathrel{%\n    \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=21AA}%\n    \\lhook\\joinrel\\rightarrow%\n    \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n  }%\n%\nN_r\\times X$ is the graph of $\\rho$, then $$0\\longrightarrow(\\psi\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\\mathcal I_r\n \\longrightarrow(\\phi\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\\mathcal I_{r-1}\n \\longrightarrow j_*\\mathcal L\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{rel:universal-ideal-sequence}$$ For a two-term vector bundle resolution $0\\to U_1\\to U_0\\to\\mathcal I_{r-1}\\to0$ on $X^{[r-1]}\\times X$, one has, for every $k\\geq0$, $$\\sigma_*(\\zeta^k\\cap[N_r])\n =\\left[\\frac{c(U_1^\\vee)}{c(U_0^\\vee)}\\right]_k\n \\cap[X^{[r-1]}\\times X].\n \\label{rel:projective-pushdown}$$ All these identities remain valid after adjoining other Hilbert or surface factors.",
          "source_id": "PM-040.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $r>0$, and let $N_r=X^{[r-1,r]}$ be the scheme of nested subschemes $Z'\\subset Z$ of lengths $r-1,r$. If $j:N_r%\n  %\n  \\mathrel{%\n    \\BeginAccSupp{method=hex,unicode,space=false,pdfliteral=direct,ActualText=21AA}%\n    \\lhook\\joinrel\\rightarrow%\n    \\EndAccSupp{pdfliteral=direct}%\n  }%\n%\nN_r\\times X$ is the graph of $\\rho$, then $$0\\longrightarrow(\\psi\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\\mathcal I_r\n \\longrightarrow(\\phi\\times\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}_X)^*\\mathcal I_{r-1}\n \\longrightarrow j_*\\mathcal L\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{rel:universal-ideal-sequence}$$ For a two-term vector bundle resolution $0\\to U_1\\to U_0\\to\\mathcal I_{r-1}\\to0$ on $X^{[r-1]}\\times X$, one has, for every $k\\geq0$, $$\\sigma_*(\\zeta^k\\cap[N_r])\n =\\left[\\frac{c(U_1^\\vee)}{c(U_0^\\vee)}\\right]_k\n \\cap[X^{[r-1]}\\times X].\n \\label{rel:projective-pushdown}$$ All these identities remain valid after adjoining other Hilbert or surface factors.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-rel-marked-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite list of line bundles on $X$. Consider a finite polynomial of Chern characters of universal point ideals and their derived duals, together with Chern classes of these lines and of $T_X$ pulled from the surface slots and equality diagonals between those slots, on $$X^{[n_1]}\\times X^{[n_2]}\\times X^\\ell,\n \\qquad \\ell\\geq2.$$ Keep two specified surface factors as outputs and push forward over the Hilbert factors and all other surface factors. The resulting Chow class is a finite rational linear combination of pushforwards of products of equality diagonals and line and tangent classes on powers of $X$. The coefficients and the list of such products can be chosen by one recursion independent of $X$ and of the chosen line bundles.",
        "source_id": "PM-040.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-rel-marked-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite list of line bundles on $X$. The resulting Chow class is a finite rational linear combination of pushforwards of products of equality diagonals and line and tangent classes on powers of $X$. The coefficients and the list of such products can be chosen by one recursion independent of $X$ and of the chosen line bundles.",
          "source_id": "PM-040.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Fix a finite list of line bundles on $X$. The resulting Chow class is a finite rational linear combination of pushforwards of products of equality diagonals and line and tangent classes on powers of $X$. The coefficients and the list of such products can be chosen by one recursion independent of $X$ and of the chosen line bundles.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-universal",
        "exact_quote": "The class in <a href=\"#rel:fiber-contribution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rel:fiber-contribution\">[rel:fiber-contribution]</a> admits a decomposition $$C_*(T,H;n_1,n_2)=c_*[\\Delta_X]+\\Gamma_*,\n \\qquad\n \\Gamma_*\\in\\sum_{a+b=2}R_X^a\\boxtimes R_X^b.\n \\label{rel:universal-decomposition}$$ One can choose $c_*$ by a fixed universal rule. Besides the integer parameters and the chosen weights, this rule depends only on $$\\int_X c_2(T_X),\\qquad\n \\int_X UV\\quad\n (U,V\\in\\{K,L_1,L_2,T,H\\}).\n \\label{rel:numerical-data}$$ It gives $c_*=0$ when $n_1=n_2=0$. The same rule applies on every smooth projective surface with the same data and $\\chi(\\mathcal O_X)=1$, whether or not the lines $T,H$ are effective; effectivity is needed only for realizing the formula as a fixed contribution.",

        "anchor": "stmt-thm-chow-relation",
        "exact_quote": "For either surface setting, and any $p\\geq0$ with $v>p+2$, sum the classes <a href=\"#rel:fiber-contribution\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"rel:fiber-contribution\">[rel:fiber-contribution]</a> with the following multiplicities:*\n\n1.  *If $p_g=q=0$, sum over the effective ordered line pairs $T+H=D$ and nonnegative point lengths satisfying <a href=\"#eq:length\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:length\">[eq:length]</a>, with $m_T,m_H\\geq0$. Each fixed part has multiplier $1$.*\n\n2.  *In the auxiliary setting, $D=3K$. Sum over the ordered pairs $(3K,0),(0,3K)$ with multiplier $1$, and $(2K,K),(K,2K)$ with multiplier $-s$, with the admissible point lengths. Here $s>0$ is the canonical-divisor coefficient of <a href=\"#eq:canonical-cycle\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:canonical-cycle\">[eq:canonical-cycle]</a>.*\n\n\n*All sums are finite.       },

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Geometric genus and irregularity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a smooth connected projective complex surface $S$, write $$p_g(S)=h^0(S,\omega_S),\qquad q(S)=h^1(S,\mathcal O_S).$$
- **Chow group of zero-cycles** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The Chow group $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)$ is the group of integral zero-cycles modulo rational equivalence, where the relations are generated by divisors of rational functions on curves in $S$.
- **Degree-zero subgroup** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The degree of a complex point is one, and $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)^0$ denotes the kernel of the degree map.
- **Pure Tate motive** ([source](#pm-h-5)): A rational Chow motive is *pure Tate* if it is a finite direct sum of powers of $\mathbb L$.
- **Surface Hilbert scheme** ([source](#pm-h-13)): We write $X^{[r]}$ for the Hilbert scheme of length-$r$ zero-dimensional subschemes of $X$.
- **Tautological bundle** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For a line bundle $P$, write $P^{[r]}=\pi_*\mathcal O_Z(P)$ for its tautological bundle of rank $r$ on $X^{[r]}$.
- **Trace-free endomorphism complex** ([source](#pm-h-14)): For the universal ideal $\mathcal I$, the trace-free endomorphism complex means the fiber of $\operatorname{tr}:R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(\mathcal I,\mathcal I) \to R\Gamma(\mathcal O_X)$.
- **Nontrivial torsion line-bundle count** ([source](#pm-h-31)): Define $$\label{r1:torsion-count} \tau_X=\#\bigl(\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)_{\mathrm{tors}}\setminus\{0\}\bigr).$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-prop-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-reduction): Classical reduction and birational invariance. -> Minimal general-type surfaces and their canonical range.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-quot-pot) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-quot-pot): Derived based determinant fiber and amplitude control. -> Equivariant determinant-fixed obstruction theory.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-fixed-pot) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fixed-pot): Direct sums and fixed character decomposition. -> Compatible fixed and moving theories.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-fixed-pg0) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fixed-pg0): Smooth obstruction Euler classes. -> Fixed virtual class and coefficient extraction.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-prop-fixed-auxiliary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fixed-auxiliary): Relative determinant pullback and divisor cosection. -> Auxiliary fiber classes with their multiplicities.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-prop-universal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-universal): Marked Hilbert recursion and decorated equality diagonals. -> A consistent universal diagonal coefficient.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-thm-chow-relation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-chow-relation): Equivariant degree vanishing and localization. -> A finite weighted Chow relation.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-nonrank) / [proof](#proof-stmt-nr-effective-pair-2): Intersection lattice, blowup bounds and an effective pair. -> Positive diagonal coefficient in the lattice branch.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-prop-rankone) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-middle-coefficient-2): Auxiliary comparison and the middle coefficient calculation. -> Positive diagonal coefficient in the rank-one branch.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-thm-bloch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-middle-coefficient-3): Correspondence action and torsion injectivity of the Albanese map. -> Integral degree isomorphism.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-cor-bloch-pg-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-middle-coefficient-4): Classical non-general-type case and vanishing irregularity in general type. -> Integral Albanese isomorphism.
- **R12** [claim](#stmt-cor-tate-motives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-middle-coefficient-5): Rational motivic criteria, Tate decomposition and the Hilbert-scheme motive formula. -> The stated rational motivic and cycle-class consequences.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I_DEGREE**: The integral degree map is an isomorphism under the stated smoothness, connectedness, projectivity and vanishing hypotheses.
- **I_SURVIVORS**: For the specified Quot data, every retained fixed contribution with $\alpha H\ne0$ satisfies $KH=H^2=k+1$, $n_1=n_2=0$, $m_H=0$ and $m_T=p$.
- **I_UNIVERSAL**: The diagonal coefficient is selected by a fixed universal numerical rule before evaluation on the surface; effectivity is required to realize the formula as a fixed contribution.
- **I_TATE**: The cycle-class isomorphisms use the rational Tate decomposition in addition to finite-dimensionality.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A_EFFECTIVITY**: Treat every numerically admissible line pair as an actual fixed locus. -> Numerical coefficient transport does not establish sections or existence of effective divisors.
- **A_PARITY**: Use real-valued intersections or relax the strict bound when filtering surviving contributions. -> The integral exclusion argument loses its parity and strict-inequality premises.
- **A_TATE**: Infer cycle-class isomorphisms from finite-dimensionality alone. -> The required Tate decomposition has not been supplied by that inference.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The surviving-numerics argument gives a finite constructive enumeration of intersection numbers and point lengths. This checks the numerical implication only; effectivity, obstruction theories and the zero-cycle conclusion remain outside the executed contract.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with integer k.
# PRECONDITIONS: k belongs to {1,2,3,4,5}; other inputs return an error object.
# OUTPUTS: Sorted exhaustive rows [KH,H^2,N,m_H,m_T] under the numerical hypotheses.
# TIME: O(d^3*k^2) bounded integer/rational operations; d is 4 or 3.
# SPACE: O(d^3*k^2) upper bound for stored rows.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and python-flint fmpq; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Outside-domain inputs return an error object; malformed JSON exits nonzero.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq
payload = json.load(sys.stdin)
k = payload.get("k")
if type(k) is not int or not 1 <= k <= 5:
    print(json.dumps({"error": "outside_domain"}))
    raise SystemExit(0)
d = 4 if k == 1 else 3
rows = []
for r in range(k+1, (d-1)*k):
    for s in range(r, d*d*k+1):
        if not fmpq(s) < fmpq(r*r, k):
            continue
        if (s-r) % 2 != 0:
            continue
        total = d*(k+1)-(d+1)*r+s
        if total < 0:
            continue
        high = (d*d*k-2*d*r+s-d*k+r)//2
        if high < 0:
            continue
        rows.append([r, s, total, (s-r)//2, high])
print(json.dumps(sorted(rows)))
```

    ],
    "oracle_code": "import json as js\nimport sys as system\nfrom z3 import Ints, Solver, Or, sat, unsat\nargument = js.loads(open(system.argv[1]).read())\nactual = js.loads(open(system.argv[2]).read())\ndeclared = js.loads(system.argv[3])\nsquare = argument.get(\"k\")\nif type(square) is not int or square not in (1,2,3,4,5):\n    truth = {\"error\": \"outside_domain\"}\nelse:\n    degree = {1:4,2:3,3:3,4:3,5:3}[square]\n    R, S = Ints(\"R S\")\n    engine = Solver()\n    engine.set(timeout=2000)\n    engine.add(R >= 0, R <= degree*square, S >= 0, S <= degree**2*square)\n    engine.add(R > square, degree*square-R > square, S >= R,\n               square*S < R*R, (S-R)%2 == 0,\n               degree*(square+1)-(degree+1)*R+S >= 0,\n               degree**2*square-2*degree*R+S-degree*square+R >= 0)\n    truth = []\n    for attempt in range(201):\n        status = engine.check()\n        if status == unsat:\n            break\n        if status != sat or attempt == 200:\n            raise SystemExit(31)\n        model = engine.model()\n        a, b = model[R].as_long(), model[S].as_long()\n        length = degree*(square+1)-(degree+1)*a+b\n        twice_high = degree**2*square-2*degree*a+b-degree*square+a\n        # Every solver witness is rechecked with exact native integer arithmetic.\n        if not (square < a < (degree-1)*square and a <= b and square*b < a*a\n                and (b-a)%2 == 0 and length >= 0 and twice_high >= 0\n                and twice_high%2 == 0):\n            raise SystemExit(32)\n        truth.append([a,b,length,(b-a)//2,twice_high//2])\n        engine.add(Or(R != a, S != b))\n    truth.sort()\n    # A separate rectangular exhaustive check certifies enumeration completeness.\n    rectangle = []\n    for a in range(degree*square+1):\n        for b in range(degree**2*square+1):\n            length = degree*(square+1)-(degree+1)*a+b\n            twice_high = degree**2*square-2*degree*a+b-degree*square+a\n            if (a > square and degree*square-a > square and b >= a\n                    and square*b < a*a and (b-a)%2 == 0 and length >= 0\n                    and twice_high >= 0):\n                rectangle.append([a,b,length,(b-a)//2,twice_high//2])\n    if truth != rectangle:\n        raise SystemExit(33)\nif actual != truth or declared != truth:\n    raise SystemExit(9)\n",
    "output_contract": "Sorted exact rows recording canonical intersection, divisor square, total point length and the low and high virtual divisor dimensions under the numerical hypotheses.",
    "reason": "The surviving-numerics argument gives a finite constructive enumeration of intersection numbers and point lengths. This checks the numerical implication only; effectivity, obstruction theories and the zero-cycle conclusion remain outside the executed contract.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with integer k.\n# PRECONDITIONS: k belongs to {1,2,3,4,5}; other inputs return an error object.\n# OUTPUTS: Sorted exhaustive rows [KH,H^2,N,m_H,m_T] under the numerical hypotheses.\n# TIME: O(d^3*k^2) bounded integer/rational operations; d is 4 or 3.\n# SPACE: O(d^3*k^2) upper bound for stored rows.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integers and python-flint fmpq; no floating point.\n# FAILURE: Outside-domain inputs return an error object; malformed JSON exits nonzero.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\npayload = json.load(sys.stdin)\nk = payload.get(\"k\")\nif type(k) is not int or not 1 <= k <= 5:\n    print(json.dumps({\"error\": \"outside_domain\"}))\n    raise SystemExit(0)\nd = 4 if k == 1 else 3\nrows = []\nfor r in range(k+1, (d-1)*k):\n    for s in range(r, d*d*k+1):\n        if not fmpq(s) < fmpq(r*r, k):\n            continue\n        if (s-r) % 2 != 0:\n            continue\n        total = d*(k+1)-(d+1)*r+s\n        if total < 0:\n            continue\n        high = (d*d*k-2*d*r+s-d*k+r)//2\n        if high < 0:\n            continue\n        rows.append([r, s, total, (s-r)//2, high])\nprint(json.dumps(sorted(rows)))\n",

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L-geometric-regime** (hypothesis): The integral degree isomorphism assumes a smooth connected projective complex surface with vanishing geometric genus and irregularity. Blocked use: ['Extension to singular, disconnected, nonprojective, higher-dimensional or positive-characteristic varieties is not supplied.']
- **L-albanese-regime** (hypothesis): The Albanese isomorphism assumes vanishing geometric genus; it does not assert that the degree-zero group vanishes when irregularity is nonzero. Blocked use: ['Replacing the Albanese isomorphism by a degree isomorphism without checking irregularity.']
- **L-coefficients** (formal_scope): The integral assertions are identified separately; the remaining Chow calculations and all Chow motives use rational coefficients. Blocked use: ['Reading every localization or motivic equality as an integral equality.']
- **L-obstruction-morphism** (formal_scope): Virtual classes use the specified perfect obstruction theories; a virtual tangent class alone is insufficient for the obstruction-theoretic comparisons. Blocked use: ['Substituting an unchecked equality in K-theory for a compatible obstruction morphism.']
- **L-auxiliary-surface** (hypothesis): The auxiliary determinant construction requires vanishing differences between geometric genus and irregularity, an ample canonical class and the prescribed determinant. Blocked use: ['Applying the auxiliary fiber formula to an arbitrary irregular surface.']
- **L-degree-gate** (hypothesis): The vanishing relation requires the strict virtual-dimension inequality and a nonnegative insertion exponent. Blocked use: ['Using the equivariant vanishing outside its stated degree range.']
- **L-universal-coefficient** (formal_scope): Universality transports the chosen coefficient of the explicit fiber formula, not the geometry or multiplicity of a divisor component. Blocked use: ['Identifying fixed-locus geometry or divisor multiplicities from matching intersection numbers alone.']
- **L-nonunique-decomposition** (edge_case): The diagonal coefficient is selected by a fixed formal recursion; uniqueness of a diagonal decomposition on an individual surface is not required. Blocked use: ['Extracting an intrinsic unique diagonal coefficient from an arbitrary Chow decomposition.']
- **L-effectivity** (hypothesis): The universal numerical rule does not require effectivity, but realizing its result as a fixed contribution does. Blocked use: ['Treating a numerically admissible line pair as an existing effective fixed component.']
- **L-correspondence-nonzero** (hypothesis): The correspondence argument requires a nonzero total diagonal coefficient. Blocked use: ['Inferring rational degree-zero vanishing from a relation with zero diagonal coefficient.']
- **L-tate-irregularity** (hypothesis): The pure Tate and cycle-class conclusions use the additional vanishing of irregularity and the rational Tate decomposition. Blocked use: ['Inferring the pure Tate or cycle-class conclusions from Kimura finite-dimensionality alone.']
- **L-finite-numerics** (formal_scope): The surviving-numerics lemma isolates the intersection numbers and point lengths of contributions already satisfying the geometric hypotheses. A finite numerical check verifies those arithmetic restrictions only. Blocked use: ['Treating numerical enumeration as a certificate of effectivity, the Chow relation or the integral zero-cycle theorem.']
- **L-explicit-cycles** (nonconstructive): The degree isomorphism classifies zero-cycles modulo rational equivalence. An algorithm constructing rational functions and curves witnessing equivalence for arbitrary presented cycles is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Publishing a general executable rational-equivalence solver from the degree-isomorphism statement.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove Bloch's conjecture for smooth connected projective complex surfaces with $`p_g=0`$: the Albanese homomorphism on integral degree-zero zero-cycles is an isomorphism.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

For a smooth connected projective complex surface $S$, write $$p_g(S)=h^0(S,\omega_S),\qquad q(S)=h^1(S,\mathcal O_S).$$ The Chow group $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)$ is the group of integral zero-cycles modulo rational equivalence, where the relations are generated by divisors of rational functions on curves in $S$. The degree of a complex point is one, and $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)^0$ denotes the kernel of the degree map. The Albanese homomorphism $\operatorname{alb}_S:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)^0\to\mathop{\mathrm{Alb}}(S)(\mathbb C)$ is surjective. Bloch’s conjecture predicts that it is an isomorphism when $p_g(S)=0$. We prove the conjecture. The main theorem treats the case $q=0$, when the Albanese variety is a point and the prediction is that any two complex points are rationally equivalent.

<a id="stmt-thm-bloch"></a>
<div id="thm:bloch" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $S$ be a smooth connected projective surface over $\mathbb C$ with $p_g(S)=q(S)=0$. Then $$\deg:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)\longrightarrow\mathbb Z$$ is an isomorphism, where $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)$ has integral coefficients.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:bloch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bloch">1</a> imposes no minimality, fundamental-group, or surface-construction hypothesis. Combining it with the classical non-general-type case gives the full statement.

<a id="stmt-cor-bloch-pg-zero"></a>
<div id="cor:bloch-pg-zero" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Bloch’s conjecture for surfaces with $p_g=0$). *Let $S$ be a smooth connected projective surface over $\mathbb C$ with $p_g(S)=0$. Then the Albanese homomorphism $$\operatorname{alb}_S:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)^0\longrightarrow\mathop{\mathrm{Alb}}(S)(\mathbb C)$$ is an isomorphism of abelian groups, where $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)$ has integral coefficients.*

</div>

For a surface of general type, $p_g=0$ forces $q=0$, so Theorem <a href="#thm:bloch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bloch">1</a> applies. The other cases are the theorem of Bloch–Kas–Lieberman <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BKL](#ref-BKL)]. We give the precise reduction in Section <a href="#subsec:integral-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:integral-proof">8.1</a>.

These zero-cycle statements also have consequences for rational Chow motives. For $p_g=q=0$, every rational Chow class on a power of $S$ or a Hilbert scheme of points is determined by its cohomology class. We state the precise consequences and their motivic antecedents after the proof overview.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and antecedents

Mumford showed that a nonzero holomorphic two-form prevents the zero-cycles on a surface from admitting a finite-dimensional parametrization modulo rational equivalence <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Mumford](#ref-Mumford)]. Bloch’s conjecture predicts the converse at the level of the Albanese kernel: if $p_g=0$, degree and the Albanese image should account for all rational-equivalence classes of zero-cycles <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[BlochLectures](#ref-BlochLectures)]. Bloch, Kas, and Lieberman proved this integral assertion for every surface of Kodaira dimension less than two <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[BKL](#ref-BKL), pp. 135–136]. Thus the remaining general-type problem concerns $p_g=q=0$, where the Albanese variety carries no information and the degree map is predicted to determine a zero-cycle completely.

Several successful approaches use additional geometry of the surface. The group-action method of Inose–Mizukami <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[InoseMizukami](#ref-InoseMizukami)] and its extension by Barlow <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Barlow](#ref-Barlow)] compare zero-cycles with their images on suitable quotient surfaces. Bauer’s treatment of Inoue surfaces with $K^2=7$ gives a later application, expressed through relations among the quotient correspondences <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[BauerInoue](#ref-BauerInoue), Theorem 0.2 and Section 1]. Pedrini–Weibel recast involution arguments using the transcendental motive: for a bidouble cover, vanishing of the transcendental motives of the three intermediate quotients forces its own vanishing <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[PedriniWeibel](#ref-PedriniWeibel), Theorem 2.5]. Their applications include numerical Godeaux and classical Campedelli examples, Keum–Naie, Burniat, and Inoue surfaces, giving examples for each $1\le K^2\le8$.

A different route uses families of surfaces and the actions of correspondences on zero-cycles. For a family of regular surfaces whose fibered self-product has a rationally connected smooth projective compactification, Voisin shows that a relative correspondence acting trivially on holomorphic two-forms acts nilpotently on degree-zero zero-cycles. She applies this theorem to Catanese surfaces <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Voisin2014](#ref-Voisin2014), Theorems 0.3 and 0.6]. Passing to suitable quotients then gives Bloch’s conjecture for Barlow surfaces. This extends the earlier result for Barlow surfaces carrying extra symmetries to the general members of that family. The present argument requires no prescribed family, covering construction, or automorphism of the surface.

Recent preprints propose proofs with overlapping scope. Guletskiı̆’s Theorem A assumes algebraicity of total cohomology and claims vanishing of the Albanese kernel over algebraically closed fields of characteristic zero <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Guletskii](#ref-Guletskii), Theorem A and Section 2.1]. Over $\mathbb C$, this cohomological assumption is equivalent to $p_g=q=0$, by Hodge theory and the Lefschetz $(1,1)$ theorem, so it covers the full scope of Theorem <a href="#thm:bloch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bloch">1</a>. His proposed argument studies a hypersurface section through the diagonal in the square of the surface, together with a nilpotence argument for correspondences. Banerjee proposes a separate proof for minimal general-type surfaces with $p_g=q=0$ and $K^2=9$, using pluricanonical curves and their Jacobians <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[BanerjeeRankOne](#ref-BanerjeeRankOne), Theorems 1.1 and 2.1]. We cite these as proposed proofs; the argument developed here obtains its Chow relation from virtual localization on a Quot scheme.

The use of the diagonal to control zero-cycles has its antecedents in Bloch–Srinivas <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[BlochSrinivas](#ref-BlochSrinivas)]. Surface Quot obstruction theories and localization relations were developed by Marian–Oprea–Pandharipande <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[MOP](#ref-MOP)], with subsequent virtual intersection theory in <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[OP](#ref-OP)] and its $K$-theoretic counterpart in <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[AJLOP](#ref-AJLOP)]. Ciocan-Fontanine–Kapranov constructed derived Quot schemes <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[CFK](#ref-CFK)]. We use the derived moduli and determinant constructions of Toën–Vaquié and Schürg–Toën–Vezzosi <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[TV](#ref-TV), [STV](#ref-STV)] to impose the determinant constraint. The Chow calculation adapts the nested-Hilbert-scheme recursion of Ellingsrud–Göttsche–Lehn <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[EGL](#ref-EGL)] by leaving two surface factors unintegrated throughout. This is the step that produces a correspondence identity rather than only a numerical intersection formula.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The mechanism of the proof

<a id="eq:intro-diagonal"></a>
The rational cohomology of a surface with $p_g=q=0$ is generated by algebraic classes, but cohomological identities alone do not settle rational equivalence. After the classical and birational reductions, we work on a minimal surface of general type or a point blowup $X$ of that surface. The objective is a relation $$\label{eq:intro-diagonal}
 0=c[\Delta_X]+\Gamma\quad\text{in }\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(X\times X)_{\mathbb Q},$$ where $c\ne0$ and $\Gamma$ is a sum of external products of cycles. The diagonal acts as the identity on zero-cycles, whereas every external product of total codimension two kills degree-zero zero-cycles. Thus the relation gives $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(X)^0\otimes\mathbb Q=0$. Constructing this Chow relation and detecting its coefficient are the two main stages.

For line bundles $L_1,L_2,D$, consider rank-zero quotients of $L_1\oplus L_2$ whose determinant has Picard class $D$. Opposite torus weights on the summands give a localization formula. We construct its obstruction theory and the compatible fixed theory using a derived determinant fiber normalized at a point of $X$ (Sections <a href="#sec:derived" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:derived">3</a> and <a href="#sec:fixed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fixed">4</a>). A fixed quotient splits into a quotient of each line bundle, each described by an effective divisor and a zero-dimensional subscheme. The divisor line bundles $T,H$ satisfy $T+H=D$; on an irregular surface this determinant condition also relates their Picard parameters.

Two second-Chern-character insertions of the universal quotient leave a class on $X\times X$. We choose the virtual dimension so that equivariant degree forces its codimension-two part to vanish. To calculate it in Chow, we retain both output copies of $X$ throughout the Hilbert-scheme recursion of Ellingsrud–Göttsche–Lehn <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[EGL](#ref-EGL)]. The recursion replaces the point incidence classes by diagonals identifying surface coordinates. Terms joining both outputs contribute a multiple of $[\Delta_X]$; terms with separated outputs contribute external products. For each fixed fiber expression, this procedure chooses its diagonal coefficient from the intersection numbers of its line bundles and the Chern numbers of the surface. The total $c$ is the weighted sum over the fixed contributions that occur (Section <a href="#sec:relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:relation">5</a>). The rule does not require a diagonal decomposition on an individual surface to be unique.

For a minimal general-type surface with $p_g=q=0$ and canonical class $K$, one has $b_2=10-K^2$. When $K^2\le8$, its lattice has a direction orthogonal to the canonical class. A lattice construction and at most three point blowups provide a class $\alpha$ with $\alpha^2<0$ and Quot data with the following property. For each contributing fixed pair with $\alpha H=0$, pairing with $\alpha\boxtimes\alpha$ kills its external terms. Every remaining contributing pair has zero point lengths, so its chosen diagonal coefficient is zero, while its pairing is $(\alpha H)^2$. Consequently the full relation gives $$0=c\alpha^2+\sum_{\alpha H\ne0}(\alpha H)^2.$$ The sum runs over a finite set of effective fixed pairs, and a separate effectivity argument shows that at least one occurs. Since $\alpha^2<0$, this proves $c>0$ (Section <a href="#sec:nonrank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:nonrank">6</a>).

When $K^2=9$, the minimal surface has no such orthogonal direction. We choose $D=3K$ and arrange that only the numerical divisor pairs $(3K,0)$ and $(2K,K)$ can contribute to the diagonal coefficient. Write $c_{\mathrm{out}}$ and $c_{\mathrm{mid}}$ for their respective contributions, including both orders and all admissible point lengths. They can be compared on the Cartwright–Steger surface, which has the same Chern numbers but $p_g=q=1$. The real part of its holomorphic two-form has nonzero square and is orthogonal to all divisor classes, so pairing detects the diagonal coefficient. The divisor cosection of Chang–Kiem <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[ChangKiem](#ref-ChangKiem)], with Kiem–Li localization <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[KiemLi](#ref-KiemLi)], reduces its fixed contributions to fibers over the empty and canonical divisors. These have virtual multiplicities $1$ and $-s$ with $s>0$. After restricting to those fibers, the universal rule compares the same two coefficients on both surfaces and gives $c_{\mathrm{out}}=s c_{\mathrm{mid}}$. A length-two Hilbert-scheme calculation gives $c_{\mathrm{mid}}=12$. On the original surface the two coefficients occur with nonnegative multiplicities, and the outer type occurs once, so its total coefficient is positive (Section <a href="#sec:rankone" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rankone">7</a>).

Finally, rational vanishing makes each integral degree-zero class torsion. Roitman’s torsion theorem, together with $q=0$, makes it zero <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BlochRoitman](#ref-BlochRoitman), [Roitman](#ref-Roitman)].

The technical constructions used to obtain <a href="#eq:intro-diagonal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-diagonal">[eq:intro-diagonal]</a> are recorded separately. They include compatible obstruction theories for the based determinant constraint (Propositions <a href="#prop:quot-pot" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:quot-pot">9</a> and <a href="#prop:fixed-pot" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:fixed-pot">11</a>), an excess comparison for rank-one Quot schemes (Lemma <a href="#fix:excess" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fix:excess">17</a>), and a Chow-valued universality statement with two marked surface factors. The latter applies to the tautological pushforwards specified in Proposition <a href="#prop:universal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:universal">23</a>, independently of the numerical choices made for the zero-cycle application.

Figure <a href="#fig:proof-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-flow">1</a> records how the numerical branches feed into the same diagonal criterion.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Motivic consequences

The full zero-cycle statement also has consequences for rational Chow motives. Write $h(Y)_{\mathbb Q}$ for the rational Chow motive of a smooth projective complex variety $Y$, and $t_2(S)$ for the transcendental degree-two summand defined by Kahn–Murre–Pedrini. Let $\mathbb L$ denote the effective Lefschetz motive, whose Betti realization is $\mathbb Q(-1)$ in degree two. A rational Chow motive is *pure Tate* if it is a finite direct sum of powers of $\mathbb L$.

<a id="stmt-cor-tate-motives"></a>
<div id="cor:tate-motives" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Motivic consequences). *Let $S$ be a smooth connected projective surface over $\mathbb C$ with $p_g(S)=0$. Then $h(S)_{\mathbb Q}$ is finite-dimensional in the sense of Kimura and $t_2(S)=0$.*

*If, in addition, $q(S)=0$, then $$h(S)_{\mathbb Q}\simeq
\mathbf 1\oplus\mathbb L^{\oplus b_2(S)}\oplus\mathbb L^2.$$ In this case, for every $m,n\ge0$, the rational Chow motives of the power $S^m$ and the Hilbert scheme $S^{[n]}$ of length-$n$ zero-dimensional subschemes are pure Tate, with $S^0=S^{[0]}=\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C$. For $Y=S^m$ or $Y=S^{[n]}$, the rational cycle-class maps satisfy $$\operatorname{cl}_Y^i:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^i(Y)_{\mathbb Q}
 \xrightarrow{\;\sim\;}H^{2i}(Y,\mathbb Q),
\qquad H^{2i+1}(Y,\mathbb Q)=0
\quad\text{for every }i\ge0.$$*

</div>

These are consequences of established motivic criteria. In the framework of Kimura’s finite-dimensional motives <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Kimura](#ref-Kimura)], Guletskiı̆–Pedrini proved the equivalence with Bloch’s conjecture for surfaces with $p_g=0$ and recorded the resulting Tate decomposition when $q=0$ <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[GuletskiiPedrini](#ref-GuletskiiPedrini), Theorem 27 and Remark 28]. The surface decomposition of Kahn–Murre–Pedrini identifies vanishing of the Albanese kernel with $t_2(S)=0$ <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[KMP](#ref-KMP)]; the Hilbert-scheme assertion then uses the motive formula of de Cataldo–Migliorini <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[deCataldoMigliorini](#ref-deCataldoMigliorini)]. In particular, every rational Chow class on the stated powers and Hilbert schemes is determined by its cohomology class. This conclusion uses the Tate decomposition, not finite-dimensionality alone. Section <a href="#subsec:motivic-consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:motivic-consequences">8.2</a> gives the proof.

Section <a href="#sec:reductions" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:reductions">2</a> first passes to minimal surfaces of general type. Section <a href="#sec:derived" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:derived">3</a> constructs the determinant-fixed theory and its fixed part; Section <a href="#sec:fixed" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fixed">4</a> computes the resulting virtual classes and the divisor cosection. Section <a href="#sec:relation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:relation">5</a> proves the marked Chow relation and its action on zero-cycles. Sections <a href="#sec:nonrank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:nonrank">6</a> and <a href="#sec:rankone" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rankone">7</a> prove the two nonvanishing statements. Section <a href="#subsec:integral-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:integral-proof">8.1</a> gives the integral conclusion, and Section <a href="#subsec:motivic-consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:motivic-consequences">8.2</a> derives the motivic consequences.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Conventions

All schemes and cohomology groups are over $\mathbb C$. The definitions of $\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(S)$ and $\operatorname{alb}_S$ above, Theorem <a href="#thm:bloch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bloch">1</a>, Corollary <a href="#cor:bloch-pg-zero" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:bloch-pg-zero">2</a> and its proof, Lemma <a href="#lem:birational" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:birational">4</a>, and Section <a href="#subsec:integral-proof" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:integral-proof">8.1</a> use integral Chow groups. Elsewhere Chow groups have rational coefficients; in particular, all Chow motives in Section <a href="#subsec:motivic-consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:motivic-consequences">8.2</a> have rational coefficients. We use cohomological indexing for complexes. Line bundles are written additively; in intersections the same symbol denotes their first Chern class. For a perfect complex, its Euler class with a nonzero torus weight is interpreted in localized equivariant Chow theory. Statements about virtual classes always use the specified perfect obstruction theories. A comparison of their classes in $K$-theory is used to compute Chern classes only after the relevant obstruction bundle or compatible map of theories has been identified.

<a id="sec:reductions"></a>
### Birational reductions

We reduce the zero-cycle theorem to minimal surfaces of general type and record the numerical range that determines the two branches of the proof. We also retain the freedom to blow up points, which will be used in Section <a href="#sec:nonrank" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:nonrank">6</a>.

<a id="stmt-lem-birational"></a>
<div id="lem:birational" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4**. *Let $\pi:\widetilde X\to X$ be the blowup of a smooth projective surface at a point. Then $p_g$ and $q$ are unchanged, and $$\pi_*:\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(\widetilde X)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}_0(X)$$ is a degree-preserving isomorphism with integral coefficients. Consequently these assertions are invariant under birational maps between smooth projective surfaces.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-birational"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-birational</summary>

*Proof.* The identities $\pi_*\mathcal O_{\widetilde X}=\mathcal O_X$ and $R^1\pi_*\mathcal O_{\widetilde X}=0$ give invariance of $q$. Pullback identifies holomorphic two-forms: a form on $\widetilde X$ descends away from the blown-up point and extends across that point on the smooth surface. This proves invariance of $p_g$. The integral blowup formula for Chow groups <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Fulton](#ref-Fulton), Proposition 6.7(b), (e)] gives, in codimension two, $$\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(\widetilde X)
\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(X)\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^1(\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb C)
=\mathop{\mathrm{CH}}^2(X).$$ The first summand is pulled back from $X$, and pushforward is its inverse. Proper pushforward preserves degree. Resolving a birational map of smooth projective surfaces by point blowups gives the last assertion. See also <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[BlochRoitman](#ref-BlochRoitman), Section 4, first reduction]. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-reduction"></a>
<div id="prop:reduction" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5**. *To prove Theorem <a href="#thm:bloch" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bloch">1</a> with rational coefficients, it is enough to prove it for smooth minimal surfaces $X_0$ of general type with $p_g=q=0$. For such a surface, putting $k_0=K_{X_0}^2$, one has <a id="eq:reduction-invariants"></a>$$\label{eq:reduction-invariants}
1\le k_0\le9,\qquad
\chi(\mathcal O_{X_0})=1,\qquad
\int_{X_0}c_2(X_0)=12-k_0,\qquad b_2(X_0)=10-k_0.$$ It is also enough to prove the assertion on any chosen sequence of point blowups of each such $X_0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-reduction"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-reduction</summary>

*Proof.* For Kodaira dimension less than two, the Theorem of Bloch, Kas, and Lieberman gives zero Albanese kernel <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[BKL](#ref-BKL), p. 135]. Since $q=0$, this is the full degree-zero Chow group. For a surface of general type, pass to a smooth minimal model and apply Lemma <a href="#lem:birational" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:birational">4</a>.

On the minimal model, $K_{X_0}$ is nef and big, so $k_0>0$. The vanishing of $p_g$ and $q$ gives $\chi(\mathcal O_{X_0})=1$. Noether’s formula gives $\int c_2=12-k_0$. Hodge theory and Poincaré duality give $b_1=b_3=0$, and hence $\int c_2=2+b_2$. Therefore $b_2=10-k_0$. An ample divisor has a nonzero cohomology class, so $b_2\ge1$, proving $k_0\le9$. The final assertion is another application of Lemma <a href="#lem:birational" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:birational">4</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:derived"></a>
### The determinant-fixed Quot obstruction theory

We make the virtual construction in two settings:

1.  $X$ is a smooth connected projective complex surface with $p_g=q=0$;

2.  $X$ is a smooth connected projective complex surface with $p_g=q=1$, $K=K_X$ ample, and $D=3K$.

Write $K=K_X$. In both settings $\chi(\mathcal O_X)=1$. The second setting is used only to determine universal coefficients in Section <a href="#sec:rankone" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rankone">7</a>. No positivity assumption on $K$ is imposed in setting (i). All virtual classes and equivariant Chow groups in this Section have rational coefficients.

Our goal is an equivariant perfect obstruction theory on the determinant-fixed Quot scheme, together with its actual restriction to the fixed locus. Surface Quot obstruction theories and localization relations for a trivial target bundle were developed by Marian, Oprea, and Pandharipande <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[MOP](#ref-MOP), Section 1]; subsequent intersection-theoretic and $K$-theoretic developments appear in <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[OP](#ref-OP), [AJLOP](#ref-AJLOP)]. Here the based determinant fiber must also work on the irregular auxiliary surface. We construct that fiber as a derived moduli problem so that its obstruction morphism, and not only its virtual tangent class, is specified.

<a id="der:quot-sequence"></a>
Fix line bundles $L_1,L_2,D$, and put $V=L_1\oplus L_2$ and $A=L_1-L_2$. For an integer $n$, let $B$ be the scheme of quotients $$\label{der:quot-sequence}
\begin{gathered}
0\longrightarrow S'\xrightarrow{i}V\longrightarrow Q\longrightarrow0,
\\
\mathop{\mathrm{rk}}Q=0,\quad \det Q=D,\quad
\int_X\mathop{\mathrm{ch}}_2(Q)=n+L_1D-\frac{D^2}{2}.
\end{gathered}$$ Here the determinant condition is equality in the Picard scheme. Choose a polarization on $X$. The stated Chern data determine a Hilbert polynomial, and $B$ is the determinant fiber in the corresponding projective Quot scheme. In particular, $B$ is projective. The group $G=\mathbb G_m$ acts on $V$ with characters $+1,-1$ on its two summands, and hence acts on $B$. We write $t=c_1^G(\mathbb C_1)$ for the equivariant parameter. Later we specialize $t$ to $1/2$, so the evaluated weights of the two summands are $w_1=1/2$ and $w_2=-1/2$.

Our complexes use cohomological grading: a sheaf $F[-1]$ lies in degree one. Pullbacks and tensor products of complexes are derived unless specified otherwise. If $Y$ is a parameter scheme and $\pi:X\times Y\to Y$ is projection, we abbreviate $$R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(F,G)=R\pi_*R\mathcal{H}om(F,G).$$ Thus Hom complexes in families are complexes on the parameter scheme. The notation $R\Gamma(F)$ for an object on $X$ denotes its derived sections, pulled back as a constant complex when parameters are present.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Derived arrows and classical families

Ciocan-Fontanine and Kapranov constructed a derived Quot scheme whose truncation is the classical Quot scheme and whose tangent cohomology is given by Ext groups from the kernel to the quotient <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[CFK](#ref-CFK), Theorem 4.3.2]. Here we use a perfect-arrow moduli description to retain the maps needed for the based determinant fiber.

We use the derived moduli stack of perfect complexes and its morphism stack as constructed in <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[TV](#ref-TV), Theorem 3.6, Definition 3.18, and Corollaries 3.17, 3.19, and 3.29]. For smooth proper $X$ these stacks are locally geometric and locally of finite presentation. The morphism stack with target fixed to $V$ also has a direct description: over a perfect source $F$ its fiber is the derived linear mapping stack associated to $R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(F,V)$. This complex is perfect, and its formation commutes with derived base change, because $X$ is smooth and proper.

<a id="stmt-der-arrow"></a>
<div id="der:arrow" class="lemma">

<a id="der:unrestricted-tangent"></a>
**Lemma 6** (The open Quot enhancement). *The fixed-target derived arrow stack has an open substack $\mathfrak U$ whose truncation $U$ is the ordinary Quot scheme with the chosen rank and Hilbert polynomial, before fixing determinant. On $U$, its tangent complex is naturally $$\label{der:unrestricted-tangent}
\begin{aligned}
T_{\mathfrak U}|_U
&\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{Cone}}\bigl(R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S',S')\xrightarrow{i\circ-}R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S',V)\bigr)\\
&\simeq R\!\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}(S',Q).
\end{aligned}$$ It is perfect of Tor-amplitude $[0,1]$. These identifications are compatible with forgetting the source or target, with direct sums of arrows, and with base change. The same assertions hold for a fixed vector-bundle target of any rank and torsion quotients.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-der-arrow"><summary>Proof of stmt-der-arrow</summary>

*Proof.* First take the locus where both the source and its cone are sheaves in degree zero on classical fibers, with the prescribed Hilbert polynomials. We explain the family conditions in this description. For a perfect complex on $X\times Y$, the locus where it has relative Tor-amplitude zero over $Y$ is open. Locally one represents the complex by vector bundles. At a fiber on which it is exact away from degree zero, the positive end can be split off.
